## Document 008: 1303南亞 台新

Source PDF: all report pdf/1303南亞 台新.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本刊載之報告為台新投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效性，
審慎考量投資風險，並就投資結果自行負責。報告著作權屬台新投顧所有，禁止任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。 
台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

南亞(1303)：電子材料獲利與業外轉投資貢獻優於預期 
建議：維持買進

一、3Q26 自結獲利優於預期 
3Q26 營收季增 14.3%至 955.97 億元， 加上電子材料獲
利大幅成長，以及業外南亞科及台塑化挹注，單季自結
EPS 達 4.90 元、優於預期；累計前三季 EPS 為 10.07
元。各部門營益率中，電子材料自 2Q26 的 19.9%跳升
至 30.9%，塑膠加工則為 5.4%、化工部門 0.6%、聚酯
部門 0.2%。 化工與聚酯部門獲利衰退主要是2Q26 有低
價庫存，塑膠加工則因通膨干擾終端消費。然而，EG
在中國華東港口庫存嚴重偏低下，近一季報價及利差表
現好轉，抵銷可塑劑產品與 BPA 的虧損，聚酯部門則有
美國外銷動能挹注，支撐兩個部門 3Q26 小幅獲利。展
望 4Q26， 終端消費依舊沒有明顯的動能 ， 但EG 報價及
利差持穩且聚酯朝電子應用發展，管理層預期化工、聚
酯部門營益率與 3Q26 相當，電子材料在漲價延續及產
品組合優化下，營利率有望持續走升。

二、2027 年營運動能依舊為電子材料與南亞科貢獻 
3Q26 電子材料營收達 555 億元，其中，CCL 營收比重
達 31%、PCB(南電) 29%， 銅箔與Epoxy 則各佔 11%、
玻纖 7%。 獲利結構來看 ， 玻纖營益率走升至40%以上，
CCL 則季增 10 個百分點至 30%並高於 PCB 的 26%，
銅箔受惠加工費調漲達 15%，Epoxy 則維持 10~11%左
右。2027 年電子材料依舊是成長主力，部門營收比重將
達 60%以上，營益率有望在單季接近 40%的水準，但材
料漲價趨勢會從玻纖轉為 CCL。轉投資方面，南亞科因
記憶體市場供需缺口急遽放大 ， 預估將貢獻2026、2027
年稅前 EPS 9.17 與 16.74 元。

三、高階 CCL 訂單自 2H26 逐步出貨，2027 年效益將
明顯放大 
2025 年南亞 CCL 單價約每張 500 元，3Q26 已走升至
1,000~1,100 元/張，主要受惠漲價與產品組合優化。管
理層預期 4Q26 維持每月漲福 5~10%，且有機會延續至
1Q27。3Q26 中下旬 M 系列在 CCL 的營收比重由 20%
提升至 30%，漲價及產品組合優化都有影響，1Q27 在
M6、M7 訂單放量下會有更明顯的增長。目前 M4 CCL
單價約 1,100~1,200 元/張，M6 約當每張 2,200~2,300
元，M7 單價則跳升至 4,000 元以上 ， 有助2027 年 ASP
漲幅進一步擴大。此外，3Q26 的 CCL 產能利用率達
90~95%，現階段 CCL 月產能 618 萬張，規劃樹林、新
港將進行產線整改，預計 2026 年底跟明年可以各增加
約 15 萬張/月。 
四、維持買進理由 
3Q26 自結獲利優於預期，電子材料漲價與 CCL 產品組
合優化支撐本業中長期動能，轉投資南亞科(2408)與台
塑化(6505)大幅挹注業外收益 ， 上修2026、2027 年 EPS
分別為 15.72 及 29.24 元。考量獲利顯著成長有助股息
提高，且市場因公司獲利結構轉型而 Re-rating，維持買
進評等，目標價調升至 450 元(20x 2027 本業 EPS+10x 
2027 南亞科 EPS)。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低 ， 惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次 ，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧； 其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響 的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行 ， 通膨風險則偏向上行 ，因此將保留政策彈性， 根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明， 強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢 ，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期， 台股創高吸引外資匯入 ， 推升台幣最高來到31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至 ， 台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出 ，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪 。 中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市 ， 而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局 ， 主要在6.7000 上方震盪 ， 臨近周末 ， 人行中間價為6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創5 月以來新低 ， 不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔 ， 市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存 ， 美元指數維持高檔震盪 ，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險 ， 並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢 ，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期 ；最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在102 附近盤整，帶領USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間 。二 、 日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢 ， 強調日本已脫離通縮 ， 將完全尊重央行政策獨立性 ， 暗示不反對進一步升息 。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪 走跌 。週初， 因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性 ，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160； 週間 ，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求 ， 加上美債殖利率維持高檔 ， 美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔； 臨近週末 ，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下 ，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐 ， 後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週邁入新的提存期 ， 央行連日減發存單 ， 令市場資金供
給充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週一央行標售 364 天期定存單 1100 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.602%， 低於
市場預期區間 1.61%-1.635%，較上期標售結果下跌 4.1 個基本點。 
週 四 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 受美債利率續創新高影響，週四 10 年期指標券 A15109 成交在 2.05%，創同
年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。 
台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨
警戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ， 目前油價仍居高難下 ， 通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

 2Q26 本業穩健成長 ，EPS 1.41 元：晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，
年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶
Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟階段，客戶
持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。 
 預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26 開始爬升：展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年
增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上
VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，出貨
量將逐步上升 ， 隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，
預期機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案
有 8 至 9 成聚焦機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11
系統整合業務營收將進一步提升。 
 維持買進，目標價 140 元：投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機
殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進一步成長，且公司已成功切入
Meta 供應鏈，並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認證，可直接承接
Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟銘電
水冷相關業務比重持續提升 ， 使2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以
上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

家登(3680)：EUV POD、中國 FOUP 雙軌成長，展望正向 
建議：維持買進

一、3Q26 營收創高，EUV POD 放量與產品組合優化推
升獲利 
家登 3Q26 營收 27.1 億元 ，+16.9% QoQ，+62.9% YOY，
受惠 EUV POD 及 FOUP 出貨同步成長，營運規模持續
擴大，除台灣主要客戶需求穩健外，美系大客戶出貨量
亦大幅增加，帶動高單價產品營收貢獻，另中國市場
FOUP 需求回溫 ， 多個客戶同步拉貨挹注營收 。 考量EUV 
POD 及中國FOUP 均為影響公司獲利結構的重要產品，
預期在出貨規模擴大、產品組合維持有利的情況下，家
登 3Q26 營收與獲利可望同步展現強勁成長。在產能利
用率提升、加上產品組合優化下，預估毛利率上升至
43.9%， 季增1.2%、 年增4.8%， 營業利益達5.3 億元，
營業利益率 19.7%，季增 2.4%、年增 10.6%，稅後歸
屬母公司淨利 4.2 億元，EPS 4.41 元。 
二、4Q26 營運維持高檔 
展望 4Q26，EUV POD 及 FOUP 需求延續，加上台灣
大客戶先進製程產能擴張，帶動載具需求續，預估家登
4Q26 營收 27.7 億元，+2.3% QoQ，+22.0% YoY。預
期稼動率及生產效率提升將改善毛利率，惟考量海外布
局、折舊攤提、人力擴編等因子下，以保守態度估計毛
利率略提升至 44.2%，季增 0.3 百分點，年增 0.9 百分
點 ， 不排除後續上修空間，預估營益率19.9%，季減 0.2
百分點 ， 年減增2.4 百分點 ， 歸屬母公司淨利4.2 億元，
EPS 為 4.35 元。 
三、先進製程擴產和中國 FOUP 回溫為公司成長雙動能 
家登的光罩載具以 EUV POD 為主要成長引擎，隨 AI
發展帶動先進製程光罩層數、客戶開案量及載具汰換需
求皆顯著增加，預期 EUV POD 用量穩健提升，就產業
端觀察 ， 台灣大客戶先進製程節點已佔其整體營收79%，
且投資金額、規模持續擴大，而 ASML 最新法說也顯示
EUV 設備需求強勁，為 EUV 供應鏈的中長期成長提供
支撐，另外，除台灣主要客戶需求穩健外，今年美系客
戶出貨量亦大幅增加，預期 2027 年延續有機成長。晶
圓載具方面，中國 FOUP 需求明顯回溫，且客戶組成較
過往更加多元，整體貢獻有望回到前波高峰。 
四、先進製程投資延續，2026-2027 年成長可期 
AI 發展推動先進製程進入一波新投資循環，ASML 近期
法說會表示，EUV 設備需求維持高檔，直至 2028 年都
供不應求，已有客戶針對 2029 年後訂單展開討論，而
台灣大客戶亦提及 2030 年將導入 High-NA EUV 進行量
產，我們認為 AI 相關需求不墜，可支持家登 EUV POD
中長期需求，惟實際營收貢獻仍視客戶開案、光罩使用
量及載具採購進度而定。獲利方面，內部效率提升與成
本反映有助改善部分產品利潤，預估 2026/2027 年營收
為 96.5/117.1 億元，+31.4%/21.3% YoY，EPS 
16.83/22.70 元，目前股價依 2026 年 EPS 推估，約位
於 30.5 倍本益比，維持買進評等，目標價615 元(35.0x 
3Q26-2Q27PER)。

時碩 (4566  TT)  Trading Buy

2H26 營運明顯增長 ，投資建議 Trading Buy 。  
投 資 建 議 Trading Buy ： 雖 目 前 時碩 (4566 TT) 處於 CB 緘 默 期 ，
未 提 供 明 確 展 望 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 產 品 出 貨 增 長 ，3Q26 營
運 已 明 顯 攀 升 。 考 量 2026 、 2027 年 營 運 動 能 向 上 ， 投 資 建 議
Trading Buy ， 目 標 價 92 元 (以 202 7 年 EPS 及 PE  17 倍 評 價 )。  
時 碩 積 極 布 局 高 成 長 潛 力 的 利 基 型 市 場 ， 包 含 液 冷 散 熱 系 統 、
人 形 機 器 人 零 組 件 ： 液冷散熱 方面， 時 碩 致 力 於 提 供 一 站 式 的
全 方 位 熱 管 理 解 決 方 案 ， 其 產 品 線 涵 蓋 快 接 頭 、 分 歧 管 、 冷 卻
液 分 配 單 元 、 微 通 道 板 及 技 術 門 檻 較 高 的 水 冷 板 ， 並 能 提 供 不
鏽 鋼 與 銅 等 多 樣 材 質 。公司 已 成 功 獲 得 美 、 日 系 液 冷 系 統 大 廠
訂單，且於 2H26 放量 出貨 ， 預估 散 熱 產 品 佔 202 6 年 營 收 比 重
有望 接近 10% 。 人 形 機 器 人方 面 ， 時 碩 積 極 拓 展 此 領 域 的 關 鍵
零 組 件 業 務 ， 產 品 涵 蓋 諧 波 減 速 機 關 鍵 零 組 件 、 機 器 手 臂 關 節
精 密 機 械 加 工 零 件 。 時 碩 目 前 已 打 入 美 國 與 中 國 廠 商 的 供 應 鏈
體 系 ； 中 國 無 錫 廠 藉 由 地 緣 優 勢 積 極 切 入 關 節 零 件 製 造 。 泰 國
春 武 里 新 廠也 規 劃 將 產 線 逐 步 延 伸 至 機 器 人 相 關 應 用 領 域 。 
3Q26 營 收 明 顯 躍 升，預估 2026 、2027 年 營 運 動 能 向 上：雖 1H26
獲 利 表 現 平 平 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 應 用 出 貨 明 顯 增 長 帶 動 ，
3Q26 營收 18.18 億元， QoQ+23.8 6%、 YoY+43.23% ； 且 出 貨 動
能 有 望 延 伸 至 4Q26 。2026 年 方 面 ，汽 車 、 航 太 、 工 業 三 大 領 域
皆有望較 2025 年 增 長。汽 車 應 用 方 面， 雖 中 國 市 場 競 爭 仍 然 激
烈，但 因 切 入 新 能 源 車 領 域 且 客 戶 需 求 帶 動 下，營 收穩 定 增 長。
航太領域 方面， 天 陽 航 太 已 通 過 多 項 航 太 認 證 ， 成 功 打 入 全 球
前 兩 大 飛 機 製 造 商 及 Tier 1 供 應 商 ， 預 估 營 收 將維 持 高 速 成 長
態勢 。 工 業 應 用 方 面 ， 既 有 領 域 有 望 較2025 年 改 善 ， 且 新 成 長
動 能 將 來 自 於 液 冷 散 熱 相 關 產 品 及 人 形 機 器 人 零 組 件 。 預 估
2026 年 營 收 66.00 億 元 ，YoY+ 32.62 %， 毛 利 率 維 持 高 檔 ， 營 業
利益 YoY+ 97.15 %。 預 估 稅 後 純 益 3.71 億 元 ， YoY+ 148.83 %，
EPS 3.36 元。 隨 著 航 太 、 散 熱 應 用 持 續 發 酵 ， 且 泰 國 新 廠 建 置
完 成 ， 預 估 2027 年 營 收 、 獲 利 動 能 向 上 。

  時碩 (4566  TT) E SG ：  
時碩的 ESG 政 策 由 「 永 續 發 展 暨 風 險 管 理 委 員 會 」 統 籌 ， 並 由 「 風 險 管 理
推 動 委 員 會 」 負 責 執 行 與 追 蹤，將 氣 候 變 遷 及 各 項 永 續 議 題 納 入 公 司 整 體 策
略 與 風 險 管 理 流 程 中。在 環 境 面，公 司 依 循 國 際 標 準 進 行 溫 室 氣 體 盤 查 與 第
三 方 確 信 ， 並 透 過 節 能 設 備 導 入 、 能 源 管 理 與 監 測 系 統 、 製 程 優 化 、 太 陽 能
及綠電等多元措施推動減碳與能源效率提升，同時強化水資源與廢棄物管
理 ， 藉 由 回 收 再 利 用 、 廢 水 循 環 及 資 源 化 設 備 ， 落 實 減 量 及 循 環 經 濟 ； 原 物
料 與 包 材 則 遵 循 RoHS 、REACH 等 規 範，降 低 製 程 對 環 境 的 衝 擊。社 會 面 方
面，時 碩 重 視 員 工 薪 資 福 利、培 訓 與 職 涯 發 展，推 動 性 別 平 等 與 管 理 階 層 多
元 化，並 建 立 職 業 安 全 衛 生 制 度 及 教 育 訓 練，以 降 低 職 災 風 險；在 供 應 鏈 則
透 過 定 期 稽 核 與 評 分 機 制，提 升 供 應 商 在 品 質、交 期 與 服 務 上 的 表 現，並 以
產 品 品 質 與 安 全 為 核 心 ， 確 保 生 產 與 產 品 符 合 相 關 要 求 。 同 時 ， 公 司 以「 企
業 公 民 」 自 我 定 位，投 入 社 會 公 益 與 在 地 關 懷，減 少 營 運 對 社 區 的 負 面 影 響，
並 維 持 完 善 的 資 訊 安 全 管 理 以 避 免 資 訊 外 洩。在 治 理 面，時 碩 建 構 包 含 獨 立
董 事 與 女 性 董 事 的 董 事 會 結 構，設 置 薪 酬 與 審 計 等 功 能 性 委 員 會，訂 定 涵 蓋
營 運 、 財 務、 法 遵 、 環 境 與 氣 候 變 遷 、 人 資 與 人 權 等 面 向 的 風 險 管 理 政 策 ，
並 透 過 法 說 會、網 站 與 永 續 報 告 與 利 害 關 係 人溝 通，同 時 積 極 參 與 各 項 ESG
獎 項 評 選 ， 以 強 化 公 司 治 理 與 永 續 形 象 。

6 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15 %

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

6274 台燿 
(Taiwan Union Technology Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/08 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收符合預期， 毛利率受 1H26 漲價提升 ， 預期 3Q26 毛利
將順利反應成本上漲與優化之產品組合 ， 毛利率預估提升 3%+ 。  
 AI 伺服器帶動高階銅箔基板需求強勁，同時驅動高階網路交換
器升級動能，銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸
續採用 CCL M8、M9 規格。 
 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星，所採用之銅
箔基板升級至 M8 等級；以及，1.6T 光模組需求量增帶動銅箔
基板需求量增。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報符合市場預期，3Q26 營收優於預期  
台燿 2Q26 營收為 143.01 億元，+42.2%QoQ、+110.93%YoY，毛利
率為 29.72% ，稅後淨利為 23.42 億元，單季稅後 EPS+7.82 元。2Q26
台燿受到 ASIC CCL 專案出貨帶動，產品組合優化，毛利率已相較
1Q26 季增 4.5% 。台燿已公告 3Q26 營收為 179.48 億元(優於市場預
期 3%)，+25.50%QoQ，毛利率可順利轉嫁且高階 CCL 出貨比重提
升，預估台燿 3Q26 毛利率為 33.21%，稅後淨利 36.72 億元，單季
稅後 EPS+12.60 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 33% 
宏遠投顧觀察，AI 伺服器與先進封裝等需求日增，高階 CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26% ， 而高階 CCL 供給端 CAGR
則是 7%，高階市場需求殷切。高階銅箔基板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階網路交換器，自 2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料；2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40%，M8 材料佔比顯著高於 2022 年、2023 年的
22%和 26%。台系銅箔基板供應鏈當中，M6~M8 等高階材料的供
應廠商為(2383)台光電為首、(6274)台燿次之。回顧 3 年來，台燿
產能擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開
出，台燿總產能為 260 萬張/月，+13%YoY。展望 2027 年，台燿泰
國廠、常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120
萬張/月，預估 2027 年底時，總產能達 380 萬張/月，+46%YoY；預
估 2028 年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平
均 3 年每年產能擴充約 33% 。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交
投資建議：買進

換器(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿
市佔率得以提升 。 2027 年隨著 NVIDIA Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+ 、
M9 材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，
同業台光電與台燿高階 CCL 新產能釋出 ， 產品組合持續優化 。 2027
年起 ， 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids 平台 、 AMD Zen6 Venice
平台採用 PCIe Gen6 與 Ultra Low Loss CCL(M7)，PCB 板層數增至
20~24L；2027 年，隨著 1.6T Switch 逐步導入 M8/M9 CCL(HLC 層
數 46~52L、玻纖 Low Dk2/Q 布、銅箔 HVLP4/5) ，預期 CCL 廠商產
品規格顯著升級。

三.  台燿高階網路 Switch 板材、低軌衛星板材、1.6T 光
模塊板材，27 年規格朝向高階型號發展 
展望 2H26，在高階 Switch 升級方面，800G Switch 採用 M8、M9 
CCL 規格，2H26 台燿將新增高階 Switch 客戶，隨著 AI 伺服器、
高階網路交換器產業發展朝向 800G、1.6T Switch 升級趨勢帶動之
下， 
銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸續採用 CCL M8、
M9 規格。此外，2H26 開始 800G 與 1.6T 光纖收發模組(採用 M8、
M9 CCL)開始放量，AI 資料中心帶動光纖收發模組需求，400G 以
上光纖收發模組出貨量 2026 年 1 億個、2027 年 1.45 億個、2028 年
達到 1.6~1.65 億個；在中系系統廠商積極拉貨之下，1.6T 光纖收
發模組 2026/2027/2028 年估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M ， 1.6T 光纖
收發模組，於 2 年內呈倍數增長。低軌大型 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火
箭搭載低軌衛星 ， 所採用之銅箔基板升級至 M8 等級 ， 材料損耗要
求降低，訊號傳出完整性標準提高，對於 CCL、PCB(HDI)、玻纖
與銅箔等規格預期同步升級，推升材料成本與 PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 38.49/66.22 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台燿為網
通、高階網路交換器銅箔基板重要供貨廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對
於高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，因而，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元，+108.01%YoY，毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後
EPS+38.49元 。 隨泰國廠產能釋出 ， 預估台燿 2027年營收增至 963.61
億元，+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅
後 EPS+66.22 元。

四.投資建議維持買進，目標價 2,150 元 
宏遠投顧認為，台燿營運重點在於，營運動能於 2026 年起加速啟
動，一般型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級
與相關供應的零組件商機，以及攻入 AI 伺服器、AI 高階網路交換

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.HDI 與 M8 以上升級仍為關注焦點，2026~2028 年產能建置積極 
過往一般型伺服器用板採用 HDI 設計，強調細線路設計，CPU 尺
寸變大 Pin Count 數量增加至 2,000 只~4,000 只 Pin Count，PCB 板
面積不變之下要走向細線路化(線寬/線距)朝向更細線路設計。製
程上 HDI 經過多次壓合後，PP 在熱壓多次後，每一層熱膨脹係數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相對具備生產優勢與競爭力，相對應台光電、台燿等高
階 CCL 供貨與升級也成為製程一大瓶頸；回顧 3 年來，台燿產能
擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開出，
台 燿 總 產 能 為 260 萬 張 /月，+13%YoY。展 望 2027 年，台 燿 泰 國 廠、
常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120 萬張/
月 ， 預估 2027 年底時 ， 總產能達 380 萬張/月 ， +46%YoY ； 預估 2028
年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平均 3 年
每年產能擴充約 33%。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交換器
(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿市佔
率得以提升。2027 年隨著 Nvidia Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+、M9
材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，台
光電與台燿等高階 CCL 新產能釋出，產品組合持續優化。

針對 AI GPU 與 ASIC 發展進程，Google TPU 出貨量從 2026 年 400
萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量)增至 800 萬顆，倍數增長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年出貨量達 330~350 萬顆，增長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU，Rubin 系列層數提升至 24~26L，材料端從 M8/M8+往更高規
格升級，更高層數與多層式架構，促使 PCB 板訊號走線、供電完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB

重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收 37.4 億，YoY +42.8% ，毛 利 率34.05%，EPS 12.39
元。 1H26 毛利率  33.13% EPS 25.77 元。  
 隨著 VR200 與 ASIC 伺服器專案陸續放量 ， 伺服器產品比重提
升，再加上新產能第四季陸續開出，公司營收、毛利將維持成
長動能。  
 2027 年受惠於 Vera Rubin 平台與 ASIC 進入放量階段，下一
世代 Vera Rubin Ultra 機櫃，預期將導入更高規格 ZQD ，將
有望帶動單櫃快接頭產值提升。

一. 2Q26 毛利率達  34.05% ，EPS 12.39 元 
富世達 2Q26 營收 37.4 億， QoQ -10.6% ，YoY +42.8% ，營收較
前一季下滑主要由於手機、伺服器產品處於新舊產品轉換期影
響。而獲利方面， 2Q26 營業毛利 13.5 億， QoQ -5.8% ，YoY 
+101.6% ，毛利率 34.05% ，QoQ +1.74ppts ，YoY +9.98ppts ，
2Q26 營業利益 10.4 億，QoQ -6.9% ，YoY +109.9% ，公司表示，
受惠於製程持續優化、伺服器產品佔比提升至五成以上，帶動毛
利率提升 。2Q26 業外淨利 0.3 億 ， 稅後淨利8.49 億，QoQ -7.4% ，
YoY +157.3% ，單季稅後 EPS 12.39 元。

二. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 63.08  
公司公告 9 月營收 16.13 億， MoM +5.6% ，YoY +32.2% ，3Q26
營收 45.02 億，QoQ +36.1% ，YoY +79.3% ，今年累計營收 124.3
億， YoY +47.3% 。展望 2H26 ，公司預期隨著 VR200 與 ASIC 伺
服器專案陸續放量，水冷快接頭與滑軌的出貨比重將顯著提升，
再加上新產能第四季將陸續開出，公司營收將維持成長動能。而
摺疊手機部分，受惠於客戶發布新款產品，亦將推升摺疊手機業
務回溫。此外，由於高毛利伺服器產品比重提升，公司預期毛利
率將持續攀升，且營業費用率亦將隨營收規模擴大而逐漸下降。
預估 2026 全年營收 181.8 億元， YoY +46.5% ，毛利率 35.2% ，
營利率 29.4% ，稅後淨利 43.2 億，全年稅後 EPS 63.08 元。

三. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 112.24 元 
展望 2027 年，受惠於 Vera Rubin 平台與 ASIC 進入主要放量階
段， QD 應用亦從 Switch Tray 延伸至 Compute Tray 及 Rack 
Manifold ， 將帶動機櫃快接頭用量提升 。 此外 ， 由於晶片功耗提升 、
投資評等：買進

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 2920 元 
考量到 AI 伺服器水冷滲透率持續提升， VR 及 ASIC 專案的強勁
需求，公司快接頭 (QD) 產品成長動能明確，公司受惠於各項專案
的陸續發酵，將顯著推升公司伺服器產品的營收貢獻，亦有望帶
動公司營收與獲利維持成長動能。目前股價交易於 2027 年 EPS
約 21~22 倍本益比，評價仍具上修空間，給於買進投資建議，目
標價 2920 元(26x 2027 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
富世達 (FOSITEK) 成立於 2001 年，為奇鋐科技 (3017) 旗下子公
司，主要從事電子類、電腦、資訊通信產業等 3C 電子產品轉軸組
件之研發、製造及銷售。公司產品主要應用於電腦、電子、光學、
家電、資訊、通信、醫療設備等產品之連結軸，如商用 NB 轉軸、
工業用 NB 轉軸 、 軍事用NB 轉軸 、LCD Monitor 轉軸 、PDA 轉軸、
LCD-TV 轉軸、手機轉軸、螢幕懸臂支架、醫療懸臂支架等。公司
初期與京東方、華為三方合作生產研發摺疊手機，為華為摺疊手
機的主要開案合作廠商。近年積極投入水冷快接頭模組量產，並
也陸續送給鴻海、廣達等組裝廠進行測試，同時也通過數家水冷
板供應商認證，已成功打入輝達 GB200 與 GB300 供應鏈出貨水
冷快接頭 (QD/UQD) 與滑軌， 2025 年折疊手機佔公司整體營收的
56.4%， 筆記型電腦佔6.4%， 伺服器產品佔36.3%， 其他佔0.9%。
公司總部位於台北新莊新北產業園區，並於大陸深圳及越南設有
生產據點。公司 AI 伺服器與水冷零件主要客戶為 NVIDIA 及 CSP
大廠；手機主要客戶包含華為、 Motorola ，Google 等；筆電客戶
包含 Dell 、HP、聯想、華為。而公司主要競爭對手則包含新日興
(3376) 、兆利科技 (3548) 等。

資料來源：富世達

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 四大 CSP 上修今年資本支出達 7,450 億美元 
在生成式 AI 商機快速擴張帶動下，全球雲端基礎設施投資進入新
一輪高峰。亞馬遜、 Alphabet 和微軟認為其在先前的鉅額投資已
帶動營收明顯成長 ， 同時預估未來AI 潛在商機仍持續擴大 。 其中 ，
亞馬遜、 Alphabet 和 Meta 已陸續上調 2026 全年投資金額，且四
大雲端服務供應商 (CSP) 總資本支出將上升至 7,450 億美元新高
規模。亞馬遜、微軟、 Alphabet 與 Meta 2026 年第 1、2 季分別
為 1,306 億、1,701 億美元，同時亞馬遜、 Alphabet 和 Meta 再上
修 2026 全年資本支出金額， 2026 年資本支出預估將達 7,450 億
美元。而根據 TrendForce 最新產業研究，全球主要雲端服務供應
商(CSP) 為加速建置 AI 基礎設施，資本支出預估於 2026 年將年
增 98%，2027 年再成長 50% 至 1.3 兆美元左右，年增率收斂的主
要原因，除了反映高基期因素之外，部分可歸咎為鉅額資本支出
對四大 CSP 財務衝擊日益擴大。然而，在 AI Inference 、Agentic 
AI、自研 ASIC 和新一代 AI 模型持續推升算力需求的情況下，預
期整體 CSP 投資金額將續創新高紀錄，產業成長有望從原先以
GPU 擴張為主，轉為包含 AI server 、液冷散熱、先進封裝、高速
互聯、電源和記憶體等基礎設施全面升級的態勢。

圖二 、2024-2027 年九大 CSP 資本支出總額預估 (單位 :十億美元 )

資料來源： TrendForce 、宏遠投顧整理

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP 、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google 、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型 (LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28% 更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、 GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中， Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展， 2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。 AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。 Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR 、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

3.液冷散熱成高階 AI 基礎設施標配，估 2026 年滲透率達 53% 
根據 TrendForce 最新研究顯示，隨著 AI 基礎設施建置浪潮及高
階晶片運算功率不斷攀升，單晶片熱設計功耗已超越 1kW ，使液
冷散熱技術正式成為高階 AI 基礎設施的標準配置。在 NVIDIA 、
AMD 與 Google 等巨頭的高階晶片與整櫃式方案強力推動下，預
估 2026 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片的滲透率將從 2025 年的 33% 大幅
躍升至 53%，並在 2027 年進一步逼近 60%。其中， Google 因積
極導入自研 TPU 並率先全面推廣整櫃式液冷設計，成為主要推手
之一。此外，隨著 NVIDIA Vera Rubin 平台全面導入全液冷架構，
水冷板、分歧管及冷卻分配系統等關鍵零組件需求隨之大增，帶

4. 蘋果入局帶動摺疊機高成長，估今年全球摺疊機出貨年增 24% 
根據 Counterpoint Research 報告 ， 全球摺疊智慧型手機累計出貨
量預計將於 2026 年底突破 1 億支大關，達成自 2019 年首度問世
以來的重大里程碑。隨著消費者對大尺寸書本式摺疊機的需求攀
升，加上蘋果 (Apple) 推出首款摺疊 iPhone 的帶動，預估 2026 年
全球摺疊機出貨量將年增 24% ，2027 年出貨預估年增 37% 。而
根據 Counterpoint Research 預估 ， 三星(Samsung) 憑藉 Galaxy Z 
Fold8 等大尺寸書本式設計的強勁動能 ， 預計在2026 年將以  38%
的市占率穩居全球龍頭寶座；而蘋果 (Apple) 推出的首款摺疊
iPhone ，雖預期在初期會面臨產能限制，但仍將為高階市場帶來
立即的衝擊，預估 2026 年就能迅速搶下 25% 的市占率空降全球
第二；而華為 (Huawei) 則預估以 22% 的市占率位居第三，主要由
於其在中國市場依然保持強勢的主導地位，並受到  Pura X Max 
等產品的推動。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 63.08  
公司公告 9 月營收 16.13 億， MoM +5.6% ，YoY +32.2% ，3Q26
營收 45.02 億，QoQ +36.1% ，YoY +79.3% ，今年累計營收 124.3
億， YoY +47.3% 。展望 2H26 ，公司預期隨著 VR200 與 ASIC 伺
服器專案陸續放量，水冷快接頭與滑軌的出貨比重將顯著提升，
再加上新產能第四季將陸續開出，公司營收將維持成長動能。而
摺疊手機部分，受惠於客戶發布新款產品，亦將推升摺疊手機業
務回溫。此外，由於高毛利伺服器產品比重提升，公司預期毛利
率將持續攀升，且營業費用率亦將隨營收規模擴大而逐漸下降。
預估 2026 全年營收 181.8 億元， YoY +46.5% ，毛利率 35.2% ，
營利率 29.4% ，稅後淨利 43.2 億，全年稅後 EPS 63.08 元。

二. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 112.24 元 
展望 2027 年，受惠於 Vera Rubin 平台與 ASIC 進入主要放量階
段， QD 應用亦從 Switch Tray 延伸至 Compute Tray 及 Rack 
Manifold ， 將帶動機櫃快接頭用量提升 。 此外 ， 由於晶片功耗提升 、
冷卻液流量需求增加， NVIDIA 下一世代的 Vera Rubin Ultra 機
櫃，預期將導入更高規格 ZQD ，單價預估將比現行的 MQD/UQD
顯著提升，將有望帶動單櫃快接頭產值提升。預估 2027 全年營收
295.5 億元， YoY +46.5% ，毛利率 37.6% ，營利率 32.3% ，稅後
淨利 76.9 億，全年稅後 EPS 112.24 元。

## Document 214: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

Key: 환율 가정 하향에도 27 년 이익 전망 유지. 공급 부족 심화 
Step: 글로벌 최상위 이익 창출, 주주환원. 현저한 저평가

## Document 284: CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận - Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận -  Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

Biểu đồ 1. Kết quả kinh doanh theo quý  Biểu đồ 2. Diễn biến doanh thu thuần/ngày và doanh thu bán 
lẻ/ngày theo quý

Nguồn: PNJ, KBSV   Nguồn: PNJ, KBSV

### Page 2

2

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

## Document 306: First Impressions_ RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026 (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#rbnz #newzealand
First Impressions: RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026
By Kelly Eckhold & Westpac New Zealand Economics
12:30 October 28 2026 Share
The RBNZ left the OCR unchanged at 2.75% (in contrast to market expectation for a hike,
including ourselves).
The MPC has stuck to its strategy of pausing to assess the impact of tightening already done. There is
a clear split among the MPC members on where the risks lie although it seems clear enough that

higher interest rates are in the future the data evolves as the Committee expects. Key factors to focus
on are data on inflation expectations, the global economy (and potential downside risks there) and the

### Page 2

path of fuel prices.  We expect a 25 bp increase in the OCR in December to 3% and are reverting to
the OCR profile we had up until mid last week.  We don't think the December hike is a done deal as the
data will inform the next move. We will revert with more detail after the press conference. 
Key takeouts from the minutes below.

Media contact
Kelly Eckhold
Chief economist 
+64 9 348 9382
+64 21 786 758
Key takeouts
Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
The next policy
decision
Future policy decisions will depend
on the Committee’s judgement of the
balance of risks to medium-term
inflation.
Members discussed that information
arriving over the next six weeks
could meaningfully change the
appropriate policy stance in either
direction.
  Conditional on the central economic
outlook, members judged that the
OCR may need to increase further.
All members agreed that tighter
monetary policy is likely to be
required, but remains conditional on
the economic outlook.
  However, the future OCR path is not
pre-determined.

The medium term
direction for the
OCR
The Committee judges that gradually
removing monetary stimulus is
appropriate to return inflation to the
2 percent target mid-point while
supporting growth and employment.
Further OCR increases likely, but
are conditional on the outlook

### Page 3

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  All members agreed that gradually
removing monetary stimulus is
consistent with achieving the
medium-term inflation target,
conditional on the outlook.
If domestic pricing pressures fail to
moderate sufficiently or broaden
beyond what costs and normal pass-
through can explain, this could
warrant further tightening.
  Conditional on the central economic
outlook, members judged that the
OCR may need to increase further.
On the other hand, subdued pricing
pressures could require holding at
future meetings.
    And if the global economy turns
sharply down, materially weakening
demand and the inflation outlook,
this could alter the desired stance,
including leading to monetary
easing.

The neutral OCR   The Committee acknowledged that
the neutral interest rate has a wide
range of plausible estimates.
    Members noted that the increase in
global long-term yields may partly
signal a higher global neutral
interest rate, due to higher inflation
expectations, war destruction and
demand for capital to support AI
investment.
    Higher global interest rates could
bring forward a potential global
downturn, and the neutral interest
rate could decline rapidly in the
event of an abrupt global
adjustment.

### Page 4

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting

The strength of
economic activity
After lacklustre growth in the June
quarter, New Zealand's economic
recovery has most likely resumed
but remains uneven.
The New Zealand economy
continues to recover, although
conditions remain uneven.
  The recovery is expected to
strengthen and broaden.
The Committee noted that GDP
growth was more than expected
through the first half of 2026.
  The Committee assessed that
growth was lacklustre in the second
quarter of 2026.
Business survey measures point to
continued expansion in economic
activity.
  The economic recovery is expected
to have resumed in the third quarter,
while remaining uneven across
sectors and regions.
Household consumption has been
weak and the aggregate saving rate
is estimated to be 3.7 percent,
above the 10-year average of 1
percent.

The global
economic
outlook
The global economy is facing
significant risks that could affect
commodity prices and demand for
exports.
Global growth has been resilient but
is increasingly fragile given
geopolitical tensions, high energy
prices, financial stresses, and
surging AI-related investment.
  Despite recent geopolitical events,
economic growth in New Zealand’s
trading partners has remained
resilient and the outlook has
improved.
The Committee assessed that there
are significant risks to the global
economic outlook.

### Page 5

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  Although risks to the global economy
remain heightened, the Committee
expects global economic conditions
to remain favourable for New
Zealand’s export facing businesses
over the medium term.
The Committee noted that a global
downturn could occur abruptly.

The labour
market
Conditions in the labour market
should improve as the recovery
gathers pace.
Employment growth remains
subdued and higher prices will
further reduce real incomes and
purchasing power.
  Employment growth has not been
sufficient to fully absorb new
entrants into the labour market and
unemployment is elevated,
particularly in Auckland and
Wellington and for youth and the
long-term unemployed.
Employment intentions remain
weak, pointing to continued slow
recovery in the labour market.
  The labour market is expected to
slowly improve, with employment
growing and unemployment falling
as the economy recovers.
To date, wage growth has been
subdued, and medium-term inflation
expectations have remained stable.
  On balance, the Committee
assesses that spare capacity
remains in the economy, particularly
in the labour market.

### Page 6

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
The short term
inflation outlook
Inflation increased to 4.1 percent in
the June quarter because of higher
fuel prices arising from the conflict in
the Middle East.
Annual inflation is expected to
increase to 4.4 percent in the
December quarter, largely reflecting
current futures prices for fuel.
  The Committee expects inflation to
remain elevated this year before
returning to the target band by mid-
2027 and reaching the 2 percent
mid-point later next year.
Near-term inflation forecasts are
uncertain given volatility in prices for
oil and refined fuels.
    Direct effects of higher fuel prices
will drop out of the annual inflation
calculation in late 2027 if prices for
refined petroleum products stabilise
or fall.

The medium term
inflation outlook
Core inflation, expected wage
growth, and inflation expectations
remain consistent with inflation
returning to the 1 to 3 percent target
band by mid-2027 and the 2 percent
target midpoint later next year.
The Committee is focused on
returning inflation to the 2 percent
target mid-point over the medium
term, discounting temporary
disturbances to inflation and
avoiding unnecessary economic
instability.
  Longer-term inflation expectations
remain near 2 percent, while most
measures of one and two-year-
ahead inflation expectations have
fallen since May.
Impacts on shortterm pricing
behaviour appear to have been
contained and medium-term inflation
expectations remain well anchored.
  Currently, indicators of medium-term
inflation are consistent with inflation
returning to target.
But the longer inflation remains high,
the greater the risk of higher costs
feeding into inflation expectations
and price- and wage-setting
behaviour, making inflation more
persistent.

### Page 7

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting

The balance of
risks for
economic growth
and inflation
New Zealand’s economic recovery
could be stronger or weaker than
expected and price pressures could
generate more persistent inflation.
New Zealand’s recovery could be
stronger than expected but could
also lose momentum if the global
environment deteriorates or export
strength does not flow through to
jobs, incomes and household
spending.
  Consumption growth could be
subdued if employment and house
price growth remain weak, or if
precautionary behaviour persists.
There is a risk that inflation becomes
entrenched before conventional
expectations measures respond,
despite the previous two OCR
increases.
  Alternatively, consumption growth
could be stronger and absorb spare
capacity faster than currently
anticipated.
In addition, the world economy could
tip abruptly into a downturn causing
weaker inflation over the medium
term.
  All members agreed that downside
risks to activity were significant and
that the recovery could remain
uneven.
It is also possible that geopolitical
escalation could drive fuel prices
higher while weakening global
economic activity at the same time.

Key arguments
advanced by
more hawkish
MPC members
Hayley Gourley, Karen Silk,
Prasanna Gai and Anna Breman
saw upside risks to inflation relative
to the central projection.
Hayley Gourley, Paul Conway and
Karen Silk favoured increasing the
OCR by 25 basis points at this
meeting.

### Page 8

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  These members observed that more
persistence in energy and
petrochemical prices could raise
near-term inflation risks, impact
price-setting behaviour, and lead to
more sticky inflation over the
medium term.
These members emphasised that
core inflation has been above levels
consistent with headline inflation at
the target mid-point for some time.
  Price-setting can be a front-loaded
process, meaning businesses could
raise domestic prices more than
import costs warrant.
This increases the risk of inflation
becoming entrenched in price-
setting behaviour as the conflict and
its consequences endure, including
through higher freight and shipping
costs.
  These factors increase the risk that
monetary policy needs to lean
against broader inflation pressures.
Acting now would guard against
sustained inflation pressures and
potentially reduce the risk of needing
to do more with interest rates later.

Key arguments
advanced by
more dovish MPC
members in each
meeting
Paul Conway and Carl Hansen saw
risks to inflation as balanced.
Anna Breman, Carl Hansen,
Rebecca Williams and Prasanna Gai
favoured holding the OCR at this
meeting as two recent OCR
increases allow the Committee to
take further time to assess their
effects and economic developments.
  These members shared concerns
that high inflation could become
embedded through price-setting
behaviour.
Actual inflation was lower than
expected, reflecting smaller indirect
effects of higher fuels prices, and
medium-term inflation expectations
and wage growth remain consistent
with returning inflation to the target
mid-point.

### Page 9

Related articles
Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
But weighed against this, they saw
activity risks as skewed to the
downside and noted that these could
weigh on inflation.
These members emphasised the
risk of a weak recovery, particularly
in household consumption and
investment.
They highlighted the potential effects
of weak house prices and
precautionary behaviour, weakening
growth in household spending.
They favoured pausing at this

meeting to observe and assess
demand and financial conditions.

## Document 307: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

Our best guess is that the budget will include a hike in the Bank
Surcharge (raising £2bn if increased from 3% to 8%), together with
a broadening of the tax base for National Insurance to landlords
and partners in LLPs (together yielding around £2bn/year). Other
options, like ending a loophole on stamp duty for commercial
property or lowering the VAT threshold for businesses, have also

been on the table.
The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart
below.
How the Chancellor could raise taxes

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

### Page 8

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

Still, the point is that, beneath these numbers, public sector net
borrowing is still likely to be projected to keep falling through the
rest of the decade. The major risk – related to some of the things

discussed earlier, like larger near-term spending or surprise
increases in tax allowances – would jeopardise this fundamental
improvement in bond issuance in the medium term. That's
something investors wouldn’t take kindly to.

## Document 358: OpenAI拟采购谷歌TPU芯片，英伟达主导地位遭受挑战

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI拟采购谷歌TPU芯片，英伟达主导地位遭受挑战.pdf

### Page 1

⻓期下单英伟达芯⽚的 OpenAI 被曝计划采购⾕歌 TPU 芯⽚。这对⾕歌芯⽚业务是重⼤利
好，也对英伟达构成潜在压⼒。英伟达此前已承诺为 OpenAI 提供逾 1000 亿美元信贷⽀
持， OpenAI 更承诺 2030 年前花费约 6000 亿美元使⽤英伟达服务器。
摘要：
英伟达最重要的客⼾之⼀，正在向其竞争对⼿下单。
10 ⽉ 27 ⽇，据 The Information 援引知情⼈⼠透露， OpenAI 计划采购⾕歌的张量处理器（ TPU ）
芯⽚。
这对⾕歌芯⽚业务⽽⾔是⼀次重要突破。⾕歌 TPU ⻓期被视为英伟达 GPU 在 AI 算⼒市场的潜在
挑战者，⽽ OpenAI 正是英伟达最具价值的客⼾之⼀。
与此同时， OpenAI 已发布招聘启事，寻找⼀名⼯程师 " 主导 " 其最先进前沿模型在 TPU 上的优化
⼯作 —— 这表明相关布局已进⼊实质推进阶段。
英伟达与 OpenAI ：数千亿美元的深度绑定
OpenAI 与英伟达的关系极为紧密。
今年 8 ⽉，英伟达同意为 OpenAI 在俄亥俄州规划的⼤型数据中⼼项⽬提供逾 1000 亿美元的信贷

⽀持，该项⽬将完全采⽤英伟达芯⽚架构。
英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋当时在博客中写道， OpenAI 已承诺在 2030 年前花费约 6000 亿美元使⽤英伟
达服务器。
然⽽，这并未阻⽌ OpenAI 寻求多元化供应。
并⾮⾸次尝试，但这次更进⼀步
据 The Information 去年报道， OpenAI 已开始使⽤⾕歌 TPU ，以降低对英伟达芯⽚的依赖。消息
⼀出，⻩仁勋迅速采取⾏动，英伟达与 OpenAI 随后达成新协议。 OpenAI 发⾔⼈当时表⽰，公
司 " ⽬前没有专⻔使⽤ TPU 的计划 " 。
如今，这⼀表态已成过去式。
此次 OpenAI 发布的招聘启事显⽰，相关⼯作仍处于早期阶段。职位描述称，该职位将对整个优
化⼯作拥有 " ⼴泛的技术主导权 " ，并注明 " 欢迎在 TPU 训练或推理⽅⾯积累的经验，以及在 TPU
上优化真实⼯作负载的扎实基础 " 。
⾕歌 TPU ：从⾃⽤到对外扩张
⾕歌开发 TPU 已逾⼗年，并声称其效率优于英伟达 GPU 。
多年来，⾕歌通过⾃有云设施向外部客⼾出租 TPU ，⽤⼾涵盖 Anthropic （曾⽤ TPU 训练模型）、

苹果，以及 Citadel Securities 等交易公司和众多初创企业。近期，⾕歌开始向其他数据中⼼运营
商直接销售 TPU ，最初以 Fluidstack 等新兴云服务商为切⼊点。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 365: Preview of RBNZ October 2026 Monetary Policy Review (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Preview of RBNZ October 2026 Monetary Policy Review (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Following on?
• We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps
to 3% at the 28 October Review.
• A vote seems very possible as differences in
view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook
has really shifted in recent weeks.
• Data since September show a higher outlook
for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP
starting point, but increased risks to domestic
consumption and residential investment. On

balance, the medium-term inflation picture
will probably be judged to have worsened
slightly given recent movements in energy
prices and the exchange rate.
• Forward guidance will likely leave open
the option to raise the OCR further at the
December Monetary Policy Statement without
presenting it as a sure thing.
• Given the ‘pause’ signal in September, the
press release will likely emphasise the more
concerning short-term inflation picture driven

by the lower exchange rate and higher energy
prices. The tightening in financial conditions
through higher short- and long-term interest
rates, as well as lingering spare capacity, will
be presented as offsets that means the MPC
retains confidence that inflation will return
towards 2% over time.
• We still think a more measured tightening
approach is appropriate from here. Raising the
OCR at every meeting until something ‘breaks’
could be a risky strategy if downside growth

concerns crystalise.
• Nonetheless, the expected hike will bring the
OCR to the top of the RBNZ’s neutral range
more quickly. We now think the OCR will hit
3.5% a meeting earlier in February. Thereafter
we expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, lifting the OCR to a
peak of 4%.

Forecasts
RBNZ decision and communication.
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. As there will likely be differences
in perspective about recent developments in energy
prices and the exchange rate, we think it is quite possible
that the decision will be reached after a formal vote,
rather than by consensus.
As the RBNZ will not be presenting new forecasts (aside

perhaps from updated short-term CPI forecasts) we will
not see an updated OCR forecast track. Rather we expect
the RBNZ will aim to leave their options open at the next
meeting in December. As in September, we expect the
RBNZ to note that conditional on the central economic
22 Oct 2026 | Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of RBNZ October 2026
Monetary Policy Review

(Wednesday 28 October, NZT 2pm).

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
outlook, the OCR may need to increase further (the
September projection had portrayed an OCR eventually
rising to 3.25%). The RBNZ will emphasise the future OCR
path is not predetermined.
In September, the MPC agreed that further tightening in
policy was likely required. The bulk of the MPC saw upside
risks to the inflation outlook while two members (Conway
and Hansen) thought the risks were balanced. Recent
developments will likely not have made those seeing

upside risks to inflation more comfortable, while the
more dovish MPC members will likely still see the growth
downside risks given continued softness in house prices
and the mounting pressure on real incomes. Indeed, all
MPC members saw some degree of downside risks to
growth which likely remain relevant. All these messages
should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC will likely continue to present their decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July, where

a higher OCR now is insurance against a much higher OCR
later should upside medium-term inflation risks arise. We
doubt the objective will be to push market interest rates
materially higher.
The exchange rate will likely have greater prominence
at this Review, as it did at the July meeting. The TWI is
just over 3% lower than previously assumed which – if
sustained – will add some inflation pressure over the
next few quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted

the RBNZ’s medium term outlook for inflation. So, the
discussion will seek to link the implications of the lower
exchange rate for short term inflation projections with the
risk this spills over into medium term inflation. We doubt
the RBNZ will be trying to actively support the exchange
rate in any meaningful way, and indeed the lower
exchange rate may be presented as something supportive
of the medium-term growth profile, reducing risks there.
Financial conditions more broadly are likely to receive

some attention. While the exchange rate is lower than
assumed, there have also been marked increases in
wholesale long-term interest rates and (to a lesser extent)
mortgage rates. The impact of higher interest rates on
managing medium term inflation risks will likely be noted.
The RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that at this
stage there is little evidence of second-round inflation
impacts coming from the Iran war and the associated rise
in energy prices. We note that most other central banks

are drawing a similar judgement at this point. Today’s CPI
might be consistent with that judgement, although core
inflation measures have increased and will likely be noted
by the more hawkish members of the MPC. We expect the
RBNZ to point to the risks that such persistent inflation
pressures might emerge at some point. But that would
depend on the duration of the oil price shock. We expect
the RBNZ to continue to have the assumption that the
shock dissipates and that energy prices and hence CPI

inflation will be appreciably lower in the medium term.

RBNZ target range
We expect the RBNZ to note the labour market remains
cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Still-weak employment growth compared to labour force

growth, weak job vacancies growth and low levels of
labour market turnover are in line with this view and could
be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge a still-solid
global growth outlook but with significant downside risks
looking forward given the Iran war and concerns around
the sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Implications for Westpac’s forecast profile
for the OCR.
The expected hike will bring the OCR to the top of the

range of the RBNZ’s neutral rate estimates more quickly.
It seems likely that the OCR will hit 3.5% a meeting earlier
now. Beyond the pause that we had previously expected
at next week’s meeting, we had expected the RBNZ would
be looking to slow the pace of OCR increases once it had
reached 3.5%. We retain the latter expectation, so this
earlier hike also implies that the pause will come a little
earlier, after two further 25bp hikes at the December

2026 and February 2027 reviews.
We continue to see further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings – taking the OCR to a peak
of 4.0% – as it’s likely that more restrictive conditions
will be required to push core inflation back sustainably
to 2%. Needless to say, the path of the OCR in 2027 will
depend on the evolution of numerous factors that remain
very uncertain, not least being developments in the
Middle East.
Arguments in favour of a hike.

• The ongoing conflict in the Middle East and related
developments in global energy markets point to upside
risk for headline inflation, at least in the near term.
Westpac’s forecasts for headline inflation are now
at 2.5% or above for most of 2027 now. Upside risks
to core inflation and inflation expectations also may
emerge the longer the oil price remains above $90-
100/bbl.

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• The exchange rate is notably weaker in recent months
as global interest rates have been rising, and the NZ
OCR is low compared to Australian and US settings.
Financial markets even envisage further interest rate
increases in Europe given the high levels of inflation
implied by their September adverse scenarios (3.2%
2027 HICP inflation vs 2.5% baseline). If the NZ OCR
doesn’t keep pace with global trends, then the

risk is for more inflation generated by a lower NZD
exchange rate.
• The ‘real’ OCR rate is relatively low – plausibly around
-1 to -2 % based on headline inflation projections.
Based on core CPI levels the real policy rate is likely
negative. An OCR in the 3.25-3.5% range would deliver
positive real rates based on core CPI forecasts.
• The economy has grown above trend in the last year
reflecting strong export sector incomes and the
lagged impact of past low OCR settings. The output

gap is likely still negative but is closing. Relying on a
prolonged negative output gap to bring core inflation
pressures down could be risky.
• The areas of economic strength in the last year are not
yet waning. Agriculture sector returns remain strong,
while global growth forecasts are being revised up as
the AI investment boom proceeds. The construction
sector is picking up, as is the manufacturing sector.
Tourism is strong, supported by an advantageous

exchange rate.
• Market pricing leans strongly towards an OCR hike at
this meeting. It may be less disruptive to go along with
market pricing, especially given the RBNZ’s forecasts
imply two further OCR increases including one by
year-end. Moving now versus six weeks later is of little
consequence from a macro view. A smaller ‘splash’ at
this meeting could be desirable given the proximity to
the election.
Arguments in favour of no change.
• For all the upward pressure on short term inflation,

there is little evidence that the medium-term inflation
outlook has shifted much. Domestic inflation in the
September quarter was 0.3ppts weaker than the RBNZ
had expected, which should be a source of comfort.
In addition, energy and exchange rate inflation
impacts on inflation occur too early for the MPC to
offset and will be what they will be. And measures of
pricing behaviour and inflation expectations in recent
consumer and business surveys seem benign given the

scale of recent energy price increases.
• Firms report pressure on margins and an inability
to set prices high enough to recoup cost increases.
Profitability is struggling which clouds the outlook.
Firms expect better times ahead – perhaps because
they think energy prices will fall – but note tougher
trading conditions now. Hiring still seems modest
albeit positive. It does not appear that hiring is yet
strong enough to reduce the high unemployment rate.

• Housing market trends look quite weak and seem
set to constrain consumer spending and residential
investment. Despite very low mortgage rates in the
last year, house sales have declined and house prices
are flat to falling. It seems unlikely we will see a change
in these weak trends soon – especially if interest
rates continue to rise. The result is we are not seeing
a ‘consumption rich’ recovery and growth is relatively
unbalanced. There could be additional risks to the

sustainability of this recovery should external demand
suddenly weaken for some reason.
How might the election play into the
decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not
be politically motivated. However, the election outcome
is close. And should it result in a Labour Party-led
coalition, it seems likely the new government would adopt
the Labour Party’s promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual

mandate. That would require the MPC to weigh the degree
of labour market slack more highly in its deliberations. As
the degree of labour market slack currently seems high,
a new mandate might justify the MPC taking more time
to return inflation to the middle of the target range (our
earlier note discusses this issue). This could figure into
the deliberations of some MPC members if the decision
between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it now is

viewed as finely balanced.
Scenarios.
Our core scenario, with perhaps around a 60%
probability, is that the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp
and signals the December meeting is live but without
committing to a markedly tighter future stance than
presented in September. Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the OCR increase to 3% and have the RBNZ more
strongly emphasise the upside risks to core and

medium-term inflation, leading to the implication that
an upward revision of the RBNZ’s forward OCR profile
is in the offing in December. The downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) is one
where the RBNZ leaves the OCR unchanged and signals
that the medium-term outlook for inflation has not
yet changed meaningfully relative to that forecast
in the September meeting, despite the higher near-

term inflation outlook. This would cause the RBNZ to
reiterate its previous forecast, i.e. one 25bp increase in
December and one more hike in 2027 sometime.

### Page 4

4
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Appendix: Key developments since the
September Monetary Policy Statement.
Activity: The economy is coming from a stronger starting
point than the RBNZ assumed in September. GDP rose
by 0.2% in the June quarter, and along with revisions to
earlier quarters, the annual growth rate lifted to 2.6%
against the RBNZ’s forecast of 2.1%. Against this, recent
high-frequency data and the resurgence in fuel prices
presents some downside risk to the RBNZ’s growth

forecasts for the second half of the year. The RBNZ’s
Nowcast estimates of growth have drifted lower in recent
weeks, and while these are not the same as the RBNZ’s
official forecasts, they may be a useful guide as to the
direction of change.
Sentiment-based activity indicators: The NZIER’s
Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion was mixed. Firms
were more optimistic about the coming months, but
recent performance has been more subdued and
continues to fall short of expectations. Hiring and

investment intentions picked up compared to last
quarter, but more firms cited a lack of demand rather
than capacity as their main constraint on growth.
Meanwhile, the monthly PMI surveys have shown ongoing
strength in manufacturing, with more modestly positive
readings for service industries.
Housing market: Housing market conditions have
remained soft. House sales have fallen a further 4%
since July, the average time to sell remains elevated,
and prices have continued to edge lower. The RBNZ had

already assumed a modest decline in prices over 2026,
and the recent performance has been broadly in line with
their assumption.
Labour market: Filled jobs have picked up in the last
two months, having been flat through the first half of this
year. That’s in line with the RBNZ’s forecast of a rise in
employment and a tick down in the unemployment rate
to 5.5% in the September quarter. The QSBO showed
some tightening in the availability of skilled workers and a

lift in hiring intentions. Net migration has been in line with
the RBNZ’s assumptions.
Inflation: Annual inflation slowed to 3.7% in the
September quarter, below the RBNZ’s forecast of
3.9%. The downside surprise was on non-tradables
inflation, while tradables inflation was in line with the
RBNZ’s forecast. Underlying inflation pressures remain
elevated, with the various measures of core inflation
close to or above the top of the RBNZ’s 1-3% target

range. Since the September MPS forecasts were finalised,
oil prices have surged higher again, with petrol prices
setting new record highs. The Dubai crude spot price
is currently around $110/bbl, compared to the RBNZ’s
assumption of an average price of around $84/bbl for the
December quarter.
Inflation expectations: The QSBO showed that firms’
expectations of costs and prices remain elevated, but
lower than they were in the June quarter. ANZ’s monthly

survey showed a small dip in inflation expectations
among both businesses and consumers, although the
survey period may have pre-dated the worst of the
latest resurgence in fuel prices (the next updates will be
released just after the OCR review).
Trade: The June quarter terms of trade were in line with
the RBNZ’s forecast. Prices for New Zealand’s commodity
exports have generally remained firm in recent months,
with a pickup in dairy prices against some easing in beef

and lamb prices.
Financial conditions: Domestic financial conditions have
provided mixed signals since the September MPS. Long-
term interest rates have risen sharply along with global
markets, which the RBNZ may regard as an unwarranted
tightening of financial conditions to the extent that it
hasn’t reflected domestic economic conditions. Most
fixed-term mortgage rates have risen by 5-15 basis points
since the September MPS, but rates for the popular

two-year term have actually fallen a little as lenders
have looked to compete in this space. Meanwhile, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen sharply and the trade-
weighted index is just over 3% below the RBNZ’s assumed
average for the December quarter. If sustained, this will
add to imported inflation pressures over the year ahead.

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

### Page 4

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.

CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?

market cap here I would say.
